(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\big(\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}(\phi_{(e,f)}\otimes\phi_{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})})\big)\cong\Lambda_{\zeta}(\phi_{(e,f)}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi_{(e^{\prime},f^{\prime})}),

as desired.
∎

To summarize, we have essentially promote the results of §
5.2.1
to the quantum case by inducing a
R
-matrix on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
. We will see what
Definition
5.2
means categorically in §
6.1.1
.

5.3
Hopf structure on the quantum 2-gauge transformations

Let us now introduce an analogous set of compatibility conditions for
\tilde{R}
on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
, in analogy with §
4.2.3
. Putting
\tilde{{\cal C}}_{0}
as the component additively generated by the vertex gauge transformation parameters
G^{\Gamma^{0}}
, we require the restriction
\tilde{R}|_{\tilde{\cal C}_{0}\times\tilde{\cal C}_{0}}=\tilde{R}_{0}\in\tilde{{\cal C}}_{0}\times\tilde{{\cal C}}_{0}
to satisfy (cf. (
4.2.3
))

\displaystyle(\bar{s}\times\bar{s})\circ(\tilde{R}\cdot-)=(\tilde{R}_{0}\cdot-)\circ(\bar{s}\times\bar{s})\qquad(\bar{t}\times\bar{t})\circ(\tilde{R}\cdot-)=(\tilde{R}_{0}\cdot-)\circ(\bar{t}\times\bar{t}).

(5.5)

Next (cf. (
4.87
) and
4.86
), we require the comonoidality

(\tilde{\Delta}\times 1)\tilde{R}\cong\tilde{R}^{13}\cdot\tilde{R}^{12},\qquad(1\times\tilde{\Delta})\tilde{R}\cong\tilde{R}^{13}\cdot\tilde{R}^{23},

(5.6)

as usual, but also the condition that, given the vertical "antipode" involution
\tilde{S}_{v}
on the 2-gauge transformations given in
Definition
4.4
, the categorical
R
-matrix
(\tilde{S}_{v}\times 1)\tilde{R}=\tilde{R}^{T}=(1\times\tilde{S}_{v})\tilde{R}
satisfies the naturality condition

(\tilde{R}^{T}\cdot-)\circ-^{\text{op}}\cong-^{\text{op}}\circ(\tilde{R}\cdot-),

(5.7)

where
-^{\text{op}}:\tilde{{\cal C}}\rightarrow\tilde{{\cal C}}^{\text{op}}
takes the opposite category.

Remark 5.3
.

This condition (
5.7
) is akin to the fact that, if
R
is a quasitriangular
R
-matrix for an ordinary Hopf algebra
A
, then
R^{-1}
is a quasitriangular
R
-matrix for the opposite algebra
A^{\text{opp}}
. However, here
-^{\text{op}}
swaps the composition of the
morphisms
, and does not swap the monoidal product. We shall denote
\tilde{{\cal C}}
with the reverse monoidal structure by
\tilde{{\cal C}}^{\text{m-op}}
.

\Diamond

5.3.1
Antipode on the 2-gauge transformations

The antipodes
\tilde{S}_{h},\tilde{S}_{v}
are, similar to the unitarity of the 2-holonomies
Definition
4.4
, geometric in nature. From the computations in §
5.2
with the 1-graph coproducts, we impose the following conditions

\displaystyle(-\cdot-)\circ(\tilde{S}_{h}\times 1)\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{h}\cong(-\cdot-)\circ(1\times\tilde{S}_{h})\tilde{\Delta}_{h}\cong\tilde{\eta}\cdot\tilde{\epsilon},

\displaystyle(\tilde{S}_{v}\gamma\circ\gamma)\cong\operatorname{id}_{\bar{s}\gamma},\qquad(\gamma\circ\tilde{S}_{v}\gamma)\cong\operatorname{id}_{\bar{t}\gamma},\qquad\forall\penalty\ \gamma\in\tilde{{\cal C}}_{1}

(5.8)

where
\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\epsilon}
are the co/monoidal co/unit on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
, and
\operatorname{id}_{a}
is the compositional unit on
a\in\tilde{{\cal C}}_{0}
. Here,
\tilde{S}_{v}
acts as the identity on objects of
\tilde{{\cal C}}

Moreover, the horizontal antipodes
S_{h},\tilde{S}_{h}
should be compatible with respect to the
\tilde{{\cal C}}
-module structure: there are sheaf isomorphisms

S_{h}(\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi)^{*}\cong\Lambda_{\tilde{S}_{h}\zeta}(S_{h}\phi^{*})

which satisfy the obvious coherence conditions against the module coherence data.

Notice here that
\tilde{S}_{v}
acts essentially like a dagger involution on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
; it is an equivalence by construction. On the other hand, we will assume that the "true" antipode
\tilde{S}_{h}=\tilde{S}
is also an equivalence,
10
10

10

This is natural, as in the case of an ordinary Hopf algebra, the bijectivenss of the antipode can be deduced from the relation
(S\otimes S)R=R
as well as the quasitriangularity of the
R
-matrix
[
129
]
.
but
not
necessarily involutive. From the 2-dagger geometry, we have that these antipodes strongly commute

\tilde{S}_{h}^{\text{op}}\circ\tilde{S}_{v}=\tilde{S}_{v}^{\text{m-op,c-op}}\circ\tilde{S}_{h}.

This property will come back to us in §
6.2
.

5.3.2
2-gauge transformations as a Hopf category

Recall the notion of a Hopf internal category in
Definition
4.15
. We wish to prove the following.

Lemma 5.3
.

Consider a category
\tilde{{\cal C}}
graded by the 2-gauge parameters
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
. Then
\tilde{C}
is additive bimonoidal.

Proof.

By a monoidal category
C
graded by a monoid
A
, we mean a set/class of
homogeneous objects

C^{\text{hom}}
and a map
|-|:C^{\text{hom}}\rightarrow A
, called a grading, which preserves the source/target maps; see also
[
164
]
.

Define
\tilde{{\cal C}}^{\text{hom}}=\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, then we make
\tilde{{\cal C}}
as the additive completion of
\tilde{{\cal C}}^{\text{hom}}
by setting
\Lambda_{\zeta\oplus\zeta^{\prime}}=\Lambda_{\zeta}\oplus\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}}
. The source and target maps
\bar{s},\bar{t}
on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
are defined on homogeneous elements such that the conditions

\hat{s}^{*}(\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi)=\Lambda_{\bar{s}\zeta}\hat{s}^{*}\phi,\qquad\hat{t}^{*}(\Lambda_{\zeta}\phi)=\Lambda_{\bar{t}\zeta}\hat{t}^{*}\phi

are satisfied, where we recall
\hat{s}^{*},\hat{t}^{*}
are the cosouce/cotarget maps on the 2-graph operators
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
(see
Lemma
4.17
).

We specify the monoidal structure on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
on its homogeneous elements. This comes from the composition of 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda_{\zeta}\cdot\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}}\cong\Lambda_{\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}}
for
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}\in\tilde{{\cal C}}=\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, as explained in §
3.4
. Similarly, the comonoidal functor is given by the horizontal coproduct
\tilde{\Delta}_{h}
on
\tilde{{\cal C}}^{\text{hom}}
, via
\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}=(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}
.

We now need to verify the bimonoidal axioms (
4.88
). For each 2-graph operator
\phi\in{\cal C}=\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
, we have from (
5.1
) that

\displaystyle\Delta(\Lambda_{\zeta}(\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}}\phi))

\displaystyle\cong\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}(\Delta(\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}}\phi))\cong\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}}\cdot\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta^{\prime}}}(\Delta\phi)

\displaystyle\cong\big((\Lambda_{\zeta_{(1)}}\cdot\Lambda_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}})\times(\Lambda_{\zeta_{(2)}}\cdot\Lambda_{\zeta_{(2)}^{\prime}})\big)\Delta(\phi),

whereas

\displaystyle\Delta(\Lambda_{\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}}\phi)

\displaystyle\cong\Lambda_{\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}}}(\Delta\phi)\cong(\Lambda_{(\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime})_{1}}\times\Lambda_{(\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime})_{2}})\Delta(\phi).

These two expressions, which describe spaces of global measureable sections, are equivalent up to possibly a projective phase
c
mentioned in
Remark
3.4
, hence

(1\otimes\sigma\otimes 1)(-\cdot-\times-\cdot-)(\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta^{\prime}})\cong\zeta_{(1)}\cdot\zeta_{(1)}^{\prime}\times\zeta_{(2)}\cdot\zeta_{(2)}^{\prime}=\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}}

(5.9)

as desired.

∎

Now recall the notion
Definition
3.12
that 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda_{\zeta}
are by definition realized concretely by bounded linear operators
U_{\zeta}
. Given the Haar measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{G}
, we can endow a Haar measure
\mu_{\Gamma^{1}}
on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
by

d\mu(a,\gamma)_{\Gamma^{1}}=\prod_{v\in\Gamma^{0}}d\sigma(a_{v})\prod_{e\in\Gamma^{1}}d\nu^{a_{v}}(\gamma_{e}),

where in the edges
e
in the second factor has the vertex
v
as its source.

Theorem 5.4
.

Under the regularity assumption that the assignments
U:\zeta\mapsto U_{\zeta}
define measureable fields of (
\mu_{\Gamma}
-essentially) bounded linear operators over
(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}},\mu_{\Gamma^{1}})
, then
\tilde{{\cal C}}
is a cobraided Hopf category internal to
\mathsf{Meas}_{q}
.

Proof.

By the regularity assumed in the hypothesis,
Lemma
5.3
can be proven within
\mathsf{Meas}_{q}
. The antipode
\tilde{S}
and the cobraiding
(\tilde{\mathcal{R}},\tilde{\mathsf{T}})
induced by the
R
-matrices
\tilde{R}
are already described in the previous sections.

∎

As suggested by its structures, this Hopf category "
\tilde{{\cal C}}
" is supposed to model a categorification of the quantum enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{G}
.

Definition 5.5
.

The
categorical quantum enveloping algebra

\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
is the quantum 2-gauge transformation
\tilde{{\cal C}}
, which is an additive Hopf category internal to
\mathsf{Meas}_{q}
monoidally graded (cf.
[
164
]
)by the 2-group
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
on the 1-graph
\Gamma^{1}
consisting of a single edge loop
v\xrightarrow{e}v
, and a marked vertex
v\in\Gamma^{0}
as its 0-skeleton.

Clearly, the categorical quantum coordinate ring
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G})
is a monoidal module cocategory over
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
through
\Lambda
.

6
Lattice 2-algebra of 2-Chern-Simons theory

Given what we have found in the previous sections, we now demonstrate how the Hopf categorical structure of 2-gauge parameters can be "combined" with that of the 2-graph operators. This is done by describing the way in which
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
can be seen a a "regular" representation under
\tilde{{\cal C}}
.

Then, we will define a Hopf category
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
encapsulating all of the degrees-of-freedom and gauge symmetries of 2-Chern-Simons theory on the lattice. This is accomplished through a categorical semidirect product construction, which will by definition embed the 2-graph operators
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
into
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
as a subcategory. We call this Hopf category
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
the
lattice 2-algebra
of 2-Chern-Simons theory, which can be understood as a categorified 4d analogue of the lattice algebra constructed in
[
2
]
.

6.1
Bimodule structure of the 2-graph operators

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a compact matrix Lie 2-group, in the sense that
G,\mathsf{H}
are both compact matrix groups. We now put the Hopf categories
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
of 2-graph operators and their 2-gauge transformations
\tilde{{\cal C}}
on them together. To do so, we first need a notion of "regularity" for the
\tilde{{\cal C}}
-module structure of the 2-graph operators.

To begin, we first recall the bounded linear operator
U_{(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}:\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}((\Lambda_{(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}H)^{X})
making
\Lambda
concrete on the 2-graph operators
\phi
. By
Proposition
3.11
,
\Lambda
is naturally isomorphic to the pullback of the
conjugation
action of
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}
. By looking at
translation
actions insread, we can induce a
\tilde{{\cal C}}
-bimodule structure on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
. More precisely, for each
\zeta=(a_{v},\gamma_{e})\in\tilde{{\cal C}}
, the left/right 2-group multiplication
\zeta\cdot-,\penalty\ -\cdot\zeta
on
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}
induce measureable automorphisms

-\bullet\zeta\cong(\zeta\cdot-)^{-1},\penalty\ \zeta\bullet-\cong(-\cdot\zeta)^{-1}:\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})

on the 2-graph operators, for which there exists a natural measureable transformation

(\zeta\bullet-)\circ(-\bullet\zeta)\cong(\zeta\bullet-)\circ(-\bullet\zeta).

Clearly, these module structures come with their natural module associators.

We shall in the following collectively denote by this bimodule action by
\zeta\bullet\phi
and
\phi\bullet\zeta\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
for
\zeta=(a_{v},\gamma_{e})
. Now the point is that we should be able to recover the 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda
from these bimodule actions. However, due to the shift gauge symmetry
h_{e}\mapsto\mathsf{t}(\gamma_{e})h_{e}
present in the edge holonomies
\{h_{e}\}_{e\in\Gamma^{1}}
, we must divide
\gamma_{e}
into two decorated half-edges.

This is done using the coproduct
\tilde{\Delta}
on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
. This motivates the following notion analogous to that given in
[
2
]
.

Definition 6.1
.

A 2-graph operator
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
is called
left-covariant
iff for each
\zeta
, there is an isomorphism of sheaves such that we have the following sheaf isomorphisms

\phi\bullet(a_{v},\gamma_{e})\cong(1\otimes U)_{\tilde{\Delta}(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}\bullet\phi,\qquad\forall(a_{v},\gamma_{e})\in\tilde{{\cal C}}

(6.1)

of spaces of continuous measureable sections over
X=\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}
, where "
\cong
" denotes the appearance of module associators, which we shall suppress in the following.

Let us obtain the desired natural isomorphism

\zeta^{-1}-\bullet-\zeta\xRightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{\zeta}

from (
6.1
) in the classical case. Here, we can write more explicitly in Sweedler notation,

\phi\bullet{(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{1}_{v}a^{2}_{v}=a_{v}\\
\gamma^{1}_{e}\gamma^{2}_{e}=\gamma_{e}\end{subarray}}(((a^{1}_{v},\gamma^{1}_{e})\bullet-)(U_{(a_{v}^{2},\gamma^{2}_{e})}))\bullet\phi\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{1}_{v}a^{2}_{v}=a_{v}\\
\gamma^{1}_{e}\gamma^{2}_{e}=\gamma_{e}\end{subarray}}(a^{1}_{v},\gamma^{2}_{e})\bullet(U_{(a_{v}^{2},\gamma^{2}_{e})}\phi).

By composing on the left by the antipode
\tilde{S}_{h}=\tilde{S}
of
(a_{v}^{1},\gamma^{1}_{e})
, the antipode axiom (
5.8
) leads to

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(a_{v}^{1},\gamma_{e_{1}})^{-1}\cdot(a_{v},\gamma_{e})=(a_{v}^{2},\gamma_{e_{2}})\end{subarray}}(\tilde{S}(a^{1}_{v},\gamma^{1}_{e}))\bullet\phi\bullet(a_{v},\gamma_{e})\cong U_{(a_{v_{2}},\gamma_{e_{2}})}\phi,

which reads as the desired covariance condition,

U_{(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}\phi\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(a_{v}^{1},\gamma_{e_{1}})^{-1}\cdot(a_{v}^{2},\gamma_{e_{2}})=(a_{v},\gamma_{e})\end{subarray}}(\tilde{S}_{h}(a_{v}^{1},\gamma_{e}^{1}))\bullet\phi\bullet(a_{v}^{2},\gamma_{e}^{2}),

upon a quick change of variables, where we have neglected the module associator
\alpha^{\bullet}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Every left-covariant 2-graph operator
\phi
is also right-covariant,

(a_{v},\gamma_{e})\bullet\phi\cong\phi\bullet(1\otimes\bar{U})_{\tilde{\Delta}(a_{v},\gamma_{e})},\qquad\forall\penalty\ (a_{v},\gamma_{e})\in\tilde{{\cal C}},

where
\bar{U}_{(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}=U_{\tilde{S}^{-1}(a_{v},\gamma_{e})}^{\dagger}
is the dual contragredient representation.

Proof.

Let us use the shorthand
\zeta
for transformations by elements in
\tilde{{\cal C}}
. From the left-covariance of
\phi
at the element
\zeta_{(1)}
, we have

\phi\bullet\zeta_{(1)}\cong\bigoplus\zeta_{(1)(1)}\bullet(U_{\zeta_{(1)(2)}}\phi),

where we have used the Sweedler summation notation over the coproduct components of
\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta_{(1)}}
. By applying from the left the operator
U_{\tilde{S}^{-1}\zeta_{(2)}}
and summing, we find

\displaystyle\bigoplus U_{\tilde{S}^{-1}\zeta_{(2)}}(\phi\bullet\zeta_{(1)})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus\bigoplus U_{\tilde{S}^{-1}\zeta_{(2)}}\left(\zeta_{(1)(1)}\bullet(U_{\zeta_{(1)(2)}}\phi)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus\bigoplus\zeta_{(1)}\bullet(U_{\zeta_{(2)(1)}\tilde{S}^{-1}\zeta_{(2)(2)}}\phi)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus\zeta_{(1)}\bullet(U_{\epsilon(\zeta_{(2)})}\phi)\cong\zeta\bullet\phi,

where we have used the coassociator of
\tilde{\Delta}
and the antipode axiom (
5.8
). Now going back to the top at the left-hand side, taking a conjugation in the fibre spaces that
U
acts on, we have

\displaystyle\bigoplus U_{\tilde{S}^{-1}\zeta_{(2)}}(\phi\bullet\zeta_{(1)})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus(\phi\bullet\zeta_{(1)})U_{\tilde{S}^{-1}\zeta_{(2)}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus\phi((\bullet\zeta_{(1)})(\bar{U}_{\zeta_{(2)}}))=\phi\bullet(1\otimes\bar{U})_{\tilde{\Delta}(\zeta)},

where "
\cong
" in the second line denotes the appearance of a projective phase
c
, coming from module associativity. This proves the statement.
∎

A simple computation analogous for the left-covariance condition brings right-covariance to the form

\phi U_{\tilde{S}^{-1}\zeta}\cong\bigoplus_{\gamma_{\bar{e}_{1}}\gamma_{e_{1}}=\gamma_{\bar{e}}}(S\zeta_{(1)})^{\dagger}\bullet\phi\bullet\zeta_{(2)}.

which if we replace
\zeta\mapsto\tilde{S}\zeta
we achieve

\phi U_{\zeta}\cong\bigoplus_{\gamma_{e_{1}}\gamma_{\bar{e}_{2}}=\gamma_{e}}(\tilde{S}^{2}\zeta_{(1)})^{\dagger}\bullet\phi\bullet(\tilde{S}\zeta_{(2)}).

Note this is
not
the same as left-covariance under the adjoint of
\Lambda
(ie. a right 2-gauge action), since
\tilde{S}_{h}^{2}=\tilde{S}^{2}
is in general not naturally isomorphic to the identity.

6.1.1
Categorical definition of the lattice 2-algebra

We are finally ready to define the lattice 2-algebra for 2-Chern-Simons theory. At this point, we are going to construct it categorically from a
semidirect product
operation on monoidal categories. We shall revisit the lattice 2-algebra later and describe it more concretely once we have understood the representation theory of
\tilde{{\cal C}}
.

Let us first recall the definition of a semidirect product of monoidal categories, following
[
83
]
.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
{\cal C},{\cal D}
denote two monoidal categories with
{\cal D}
equipped with a (strong)
11
11

11

Here ”strong” means the module associators and unitors are invertible.

{\cal C}
-module structure
\lhd:{\cal D}\times{\cal C}\rightarrow{\cal D}
. The
semidirect product

{\cal D}\rtimes{\cal C}
is a
monoidal
category consisting of pairs
(D,C)\in{\cal D}\times{\cal C}
equipped with the monoidal structure

(D,C)\otimes(D^{\prime},C^{\prime})=(D\otimes(D^{\prime}\lhd C),C\otimes C^{\prime}).

Let
\varrho:(-\lhd-)\lhd-\Rightarrow-\lhd(-\otimes-)
denote the module associator, and let
\varpi:(-\otimes-)\lhd-\Rightarrow(-\lhd-)\otimes(-\lhd-)
be the module tensorator. The associator morphism on
{\cal D}\rtimes{\cal C}
is given by
(\bar{\alpha}_{\cal D},\alpha_{\cal C})
, where
\alpha_{{\cal C},{\cal D}}
are the associators on
{\cal C},{\cal D}
respetively and

\bar{\alpha}_{\cal D}=\varpi_{C_{1}}(D_{2},D_{3}\lhd C_{2})\circ{\bf 1}_{D_{2}\lhd C_{1}}\otimes\varrho_{C_{1},C_{2}}(D_{3})\circ\alpha_{\cal D}(D_{1},D_{2}\lhd C_{1},D_{3}\lhd(C_{1}\otimes C_{2})).

The fact that
{\cal D}\rtimes{\cal C}
forms a monoidal category is proven in
[
83
]
.

Taking
{\cal D}=\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
to be the Hopf category of the 2-graph operators and
{\cal C}=\tilde{{\cal C}}
to be the 2-gauge transformations. We take the action functor
\lhd=\bullet
to be the right-regular representation, and form the semidirect product
{\cal D}\rtimes{\cal C}
equipped with the tensor product

(\phi,\zeta)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(\phi^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime})\cong(\phi\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}(\phi^{\prime}\bullet\zeta),\zeta\cdot\zeta^{\prime}),

where
\circ
is the left-composition of 2-gauge transformations in
\tilde{{\cal C}}
. By strict monoidality of
\mathbb{G}
, the module associator
\varrho_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}(\phi):(\phi\bullet\zeta)\bullet\zeta^{\prime}\rightarrow\phi\bullet(\zeta\circ\zeta^{\prime})
is an invertible measureable morphism for each
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
. We shall denote by
\varrho_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}^{*}=\varrho_{\tilde{S}\zeta,\tilde{S}\zeta^{\prime}}
for the module associator under the antipode funcor
\tilde{S}
on
\tilde{{\cal C}}
.

We now need the module tensorator
\varpi
. However, in our case,
\varpi
takes a different form — it must involve the coproduct
\tilde{\Delta}
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
is generated by left-covariant 2-graph operators, then the derivation property (
5.1
) provides precisely the module tensorator
\varpi:(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\bullet-\Rightarrow(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\circ\big((-\times-)\bullet\tilde{\Delta}_{-}\big)\,.

Proof.

Recall that for left-covariant 2-graph operators
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
, the 2-gauge transformation
\Lambda
is written equivalently in terms of the bimodule structure
\bullet
via (
6.1
). The left-/right-module actions
\bullet
strongly commute, hence if
\Lambda
has the categorical quantum derivation property (
5.1
), so does
\bullet
.

Now given
\bullet
satisfies (
5.1
) with
\bullet
in place of
\Lambda
,

(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\bullet-\cong(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\circ(-\times-)\bullet\tilde{\Delta},

then the sheaf identification underlying it

\varpi_{\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\zeta):(\phi\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi^{\prime})\bullet\zeta\cong(-\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}-)\big((\phi\times\phi^{\prime})\bullet\tilde{\Delta}_{\zeta}\big)

(6.2)

are precisely the components of the module tensorator, where
\phi,\phi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
and
\zeta\in\tilde{{\cal C}}
. Naturality is clear.
∎

Strictly speaking, it should be this bimodule structure
\bullet
that appears in the categorical quantum derivation property.

The lattice 2-algebra (cf.
[
2
]
) is thus the semidirect product category satisfying additional conditions.

Definition 6.5
.

Let
\Gamma
denote a 2-graph embedded in a 3d Cauchy slice
\Sigma\subset M^{4}
. The
lattice 2-algebra

\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
of 2-Chern-Simons theory on
\Gamma
is the Hopf monoidal semidirect product
(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\rtimes\tilde{{\cal C}},\bullet,\varrho,\varpi)
as defined above, such that the following holds.

1.

Each
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
is left-covariant (
6.1
) (and hence also right-covariant).

2.

As
\tilde{{\cal C}}
-modules, we have the
braid relation

\phi\times\phi^{\prime}\cong(\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{R}}(\phi^{\prime}\times\phi)

(6.3)

under the 2-gauge actions
U,U^{\prime}
, which is natural with respect to measureable morphisms. Here,
\tilde{R}
is localized on the 1-graph intersection of the supports of
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
on
\Gamma^{2}
.

Let us explain briefly about the braid relation (
6.3
). As representations of the Hopf category
\tilde{{\cal C}}
, the functor
\phi\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi^{\prime}\rightarrow\phi^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\phi
, given by swapping the tensor factors
and
acting with the categorical
R
-matrix
R
, is intertwining iff the relations (
4.2.3
) hold. Based on the definition of
\tilde{R}
(
5.3
), this then assures that both sides of (
6.3
) furnish the same
\tilde{{\cal C}}
-representation.

Remark 6.1
.

Recall from
Remark
4.4
that, in the weakly-associative case,
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
acquires witnesses for coassociativity, which can be viewed as an internal natural transformation
\alpha^{\Delta}:(\Delta\times 1)\Delta\Rightarrow(1\times\Delta)\Delta
on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
. In this case, the aforementioned functor
c_{\phi,\phi^{\prime}}
is a
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{0}}
-intertwiner up to homotopy.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\phi\times\phi\textquoteright}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\phi\times\phi\textquoteright}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\phi\textquoteright\times\phi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\phi\textquoteright\times\phi}}

As such, both sides of the condition (
6.3
) only determines the same
\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma^{0}}
-module element up to homotopy presented by a sheaf morphism.

\Diamond

The braid relations (
6.3
) can be interpreted as a an identification of sheaves over
(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma},\mu_{\Gamma})
, which are only required to be defined
\mu_{\Gamma}
-a.e.
on the measureable global sections.

6.1.2
Invariant 2-graph operators and the 2-Chern-Simons observables

With the lattice 2-algebra
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
in hand, we can now define the obseravbles in discretized 2-Chern-Simons theory in an algebraic manner.

Definition 6.6
.

The
observable 2-subalgebra

\mathscr{O}^{\Gamma}\subset\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
is the subspace generated by 2-graph operators
\phi
satisfying the
invariance
condition

\phi\bullet\zeta\cong\zeta\bullet\phi,\qquad\forall\penalty\ \zeta\in A

(6.4)

for all measureable subsets
A\subset\tilde{{\cal C}}
.

In other words,
\mathscr{O}^{\Gamma}
is the space of (a.e.) invariants under the
\tilde{{\cal C}}
-module structure
\Lambda
. We now prove that
\mathscr{O}^{\Gamma}
inherits the additive cocategory structure from
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
.

Definition 6.7
.

By a
homotopy fixed point

{\cal C}^{A}
of a cocategory
{\cal C}={\cal C}_{1}\overset{\hat{s}}{\underset{\hat{t}}{\leftleftarrows}}{\cal C}_{0}
under the action of an additive monoidal category/algebraoid
A
, we mean the cocategory such that, for each
a\in A
and coarrows
f\in{\cal C}_{1}
, there exist an object
x_{a}\in{\cal C}_{0}
such that
f=\hat{s}(x_{a})\in{\cal C}_{1}
is the cosource and
a\rhd f=\hat{t}(x_{a})\in{\cal C}_{1}
is the cotarget. The assignment
a\mapsto x_{a}
also satisfies the usual monoidality coherence axioms.

Proposition 6.8
.

Consider the 2-graph operators
{\cal C}=\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
as a Hopf cocategory internal to
\mathsf{Meas}_{q}
as in §
4.20
. The invariant states are precisely the homotopy fixed points
{\cal C}^{\tilde{{\cal C}}}
.

Proof.

Neglecting the module associators for the moment, let us use the covariance condition (
6.1
) and
Definition
3.12
to rewrite the invariance condition in the following way

U_{\zeta}\phi\cong\phi.

It is convenient to denote by
{\cal V},\tilde{{\cal E}}
the (additive completion of the)
vertex/edge parameters
, whose 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda_{\zeta}
are localized respectively on the vertices
v
and edges
e
of the 1-graph
\Gamma^{1}
. These then allows us to describe
\tilde{{\cal C}}
as,

\bar{s},\bar{t}:\tilde{{\cal E}}\rightrightarrows{\cal V},

in terms of the source/target structure maps
\bar{s},\bar{t}
. Note that, if we set
\mathsf{H}=1
, the 2-gauge action
\Lambda
recovers the notion of gauge transformations in the usual (lattice) 1-gauge theory.

If
\phi
satisfies (
6.4
), then of course
U=\operatorname{id}
is the identity operator
\mu_{\Gamma}
-a.e. Hence for invariant 2-graph operators, a vertex parameter
a_{v}\in{\cal V}
acts
\phi
as given in (
3.1
),

(a_{v}\rhd\phi)(\{b_{f}\}_{f})\cong\phi(\{a_{v}^{-1}\rhd(a_{\bar{v}}\rhd b_{f})\}_{f}),

where
\bar{v}
is the target vertex of the source edge
e
of the face
f
. The goal is therefore to find a edge state
\psi_{v}
such that
\hat{s}^{*}\psi_{v}=\phi
and
\hat{t}^{*}\psi_{v}=a_{v}\rhd\phi
.

If we put a "pure-gauge"
h_{e}=a_{v}^{-1}a_{\bar{v}}
on the edge
e:v\rightarrow\bar{v}
, then we have
a_{v}^{-1}\rhd(a_{\bar{v}}\rhd b_{f})=(a_{v}^{-1}a_{\bar{v}})\rhd b_{f}=h_{e}\rhd b_{f}
, which is nothing but a
whiskering
operation
[
14
,
23
,
46
]
. Now let
\psi_{v}
denote an edge state that has support only on such pure gauge configurations
h_{e}=a_{v}^{-1}a_{\bar{v}}
, then the invariance condition (
6.4
) identifies
\psi_{v}
to have cosource
\phi
and cotarget
a_{v}\rhd\phi
, as desired. In other words, for each
v\in{\cal V}
and invariant coarrow
\phi\in{\cal F}
, there exists an object

a_{v}\rhd\phi\leftarrow\psi_{v}\rightarrow\phi

trivializing the
{\cal V}
-action. It is clear from the properties of whiskering that this assignment
v\mapsto\psi_{v}
respects the composition of gauge transformations.

∎

The idea that "gauging a symmetry" is the same as taking the "equivariantization/homotopy fixed points" of the given theory is well-known throughout recent literature; see eg.
[
61
]
. Here we provide a similar characterization of the 2-Chern-Simons observables: they are precisely the
equivariantization

{\cal C}^{\tilde{{\cal C}}}
of the 2-graph operators
\mathcal{C}=\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
with respect to the categorical quantum symmetry
\tilde{{\cal C}}
.

6.2
*-operation in the lattice 2-algebra
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}

In the final section of this paper, we now study a *-operation on
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
. As inspired by §6 of
[
2
]
, this *-operation will be induced by the orientation and framing properties
[
74
]
of
\Gamma^{2}
. We now work to make this idea more precise.

Naturally, this makes the *-operations tied inherently to the antipodes introduced in §
4.1.2
. As such, we will make use of 2-
\dagger
-unitarity and much of the ideas introduced there.

6.2.1
Orientation and framing

For each quantum 2-graph operator
\phi=\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]]\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
, we introduce natural
\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]
-linear measureable sheaf morphisms
\eta_{\mathrm{h,v}}:\Gamma_{c}(H^{X})[[\hbar]]\rightarrow\Gamma_{c}(H^{\overline{X}^{\mathrm{h,v}}})[[\hbar]]
induced on the 2-graph operators by the 2-
\dagger
structure of
\Gamma
.

Definition 6.9
.

We say the pair
(\eta_{\mathrm{h}},\eta_{\mathrm{v}})
is a
2-
\dagger
-intertwining pair
iff for each
\zeta\in\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma^{1}}
, we have

\eta_{\mathrm{h}}\circ U_{\zeta}=U_{\bar{\zeta}}\circ\eta_{\mathrm{h}},\qquad\eta_{\mathrm{v}}\circ U_{\zeta}=U_{\zeta^{T}}\circ\eta_{\mathrm{v}}\phi

as operators acting any
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
, where
\bar{\zeta}
denotes the 2-gauge parameter assigned to the orientation reversal
\overline{v\xrightarrow{e}v^{\prime}}=v^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\bar{e}}v
, and
\zeta^{T}
denotes that assigned to the
frame rotation

(v,e)^{T}=(v,e^{T})
.

We are finally ready to state the *-operations on the 2-graph operators and the 2-gauge transformations. Suppose the
R
-matrix
\tilde{R}
on
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}^{\Gamma_{1}}
is invertible, in the sense that the induced cobraiding natural transformations
\tilde{\Delta}\Rightarrow\tilde{\Delta}^{\text{op}}
are invertible.

By locality, it suffice to define the *-operations on local pieces.

Definition 6.10
.

Let
(v,e)=v\xrightarrow{e}v^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{1}
denote a 1-graph, and let
(e,f)\in\Gamma^{2}
denote a 2-graph, with source and target edges
e,e^{\prime}:v\rightarrow v^{\prime}
.

1.

The
*-operations
on localized homogeneous elements in
\tilde{{\cal C}}
are given by

\zeta^{*_{1}}_{(v,e)}=\bar{\zeta},\qquad\bar{\zeta}^{*_{2}}_{(v,e)}=\zeta^{T}

(6.5)

where
v^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\bar{e}}v
is the orientation-reversal and
v\xrightarrow{e^{T}}v^{\prime}
is the framing rotation.

2.

Given the 2-
\dagger
-intertwining pairs in
Definition
6.9
, the
*-operations
on localized 2-graph operators
\phi_{(e,f)}\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})=\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
are given by

\displaystyle\phi_{(e,f)}^{*_{1}}=(\Lambda\times 1)_{\tilde{R}^{-1}}(\phi_{(\bar{e}^{\prime},\bar{f})})\eta_{\mathrm{h}},

\displaystyle\phi_{(e,f)}^{*_{2}}=(\phi_{(e^{\prime},\bar{f})})^{\text{op}}\eta_{\mathrm{v}},

where
(\bar{e}^{\prime},\bar{f})=(e,f)^{\dagger_{1}}
and
(e^{\prime},\bar{f})=(e,f)^{\dagger_{2}}
. Here, the
\tilde{R}
-matrix is localized on
\partial f
.

3.

The regular
\bullet
-module structure on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
over
\tilde{{\cal C}}
is *-compatible: there exist natural measureable isomorphisms

(\phi\bullet\zeta)^{*_{1,2}}\cong\zeta^{*_{1,2}}\bullet\phi^{*_{1,2}},\qquad\forall\penalty\ \phi\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}),\quad\zeta\in\tilde{{\cal C}},

satisfying the obvious coherence conditions against the
\bullet
-module associator and the tensorator (
6.2
).

These can be understood as a 2-dimensional version of the *-operation on the holonomies defined in
[
2
]
, (4.14).

Remark 6.2
.

The geometry of this
*
-operation is clear: they are directly induced from the 2-
\dagger
structure on
\Gamma^{2}
. However, the appearance of the
R
-matrices
\tilde{R}
is a purely quantum phenomenon, as one needs to "pass" the target edge
e^{\prime}
through the source edge
e
of the face
f
upon an framing rotation. In the usual 3d lattice Chern-Simons case, the appearance of the
R
-matrices is kept track of by the so-called auxiliary "cilia" on the graphs
[
2
]
. Similarly, we can introduce 2-dimensional "2-cilia" as extensions on our 2-graph
\Gamma^{2}
, which could help visualize some of the computations below.

\Diamond

Note these orientation reversals are anti-homomorphisms, in the sense that
12
12

12

Keep in mind
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
is a
co
category without composition!

-^{*_{1}}:\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}))^{\text{m-op},\text{c-op}},\qquad-^{*_{2}}:\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma}))^{\text{op}}

consistent with (
4.5
), they will swap the left- and right-actions in the
\tilde{{\cal C}}
-bimodule structure of
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
.

Proposition 6.11
.

The *-operations on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
strongly commute: there exist measureable natural isomorphisms
(\phi^{*_{1}})^{*_{2}}\cong(\phi^{*_{2}})^{*_{1}}
.

Proof.

Let
\bar{\phi}^{\mathrm{h,v}},\bar{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{h,v}}
denote the evaluation of
\phi
on, and the 2-gauge transformations
\Lambda
in the proximity of, the 2-graphs living in the horizontal/vertical orientation reversal of
\Gamma^{2}
. It is clear from the strong commutativty (
4.4
) of the 2-
\dagger
structure on
\Gamma^{2}
that there exists an isomorphism of measureable fields

\overline{(\bar{\phi}^{\mathrm{h}})}^{\mathrm{v}}\cong\overline{(\bar{\phi}^{\mathrm{v}})}^{\mathrm{h}},

and that the intertwiners
\eta
are idempotent and strongly commute
\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{*_{\mathrm{v}}}\circ\eta_{\mathrm{v}}=\eta_{\mathrm{v}}^{*_{\mathrm{h}}}\circ\eta_{\mathrm{h}}
. Given the concrete realization of
\Lambda
by
U
via
Definition
3.12
, and given that
*_{\mathrm{h,v}}
are anti-homomorphisms, we have

\displaystyle(\phi^{*_{1}})^{*_{2}}

\displaystyle=(\bar{\phi}^{\mathrm{h}}\bullet(1\times\bar{U}^{\mathrm{h}})_{\tilde{R}^{-1}}\eta_{\mathrm{h}})^{*_{2}}

\displaystyle\cong\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{*}\circ((1\times\bar{U}^{\mathrm{h}})_{(\tilde{R}^{-1})^{T}}\bullet-)\circ\eta_{\mathrm{v}}\circ(\overline{\bar{\phi}^{\mathrm{h}}}^{\mathrm{v}})

\displaystyle\cong\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{*_{\mathrm{v}}}\eta_{\mathrm{v}}\Big((1\times\overline{\bar{U}^{\mathrm{h}}}^{\mathrm{v}})_{((\tilde{R}^{T})^{-1}\cdot-^{\text{op}})}\bullet\overline{\bar{\phi}^{\mathrm{h}}}^{\mathrm{v}}\Big)

\displaystyle\cong\eta_{\mathrm{v}}^{*_{\mathrm{h}}}\eta_{\mathrm{h}}(1\times\overline{\bar{U}^{\mathrm{v}}}^{\mathrm{h}})_{\big((-)^{\text{op}}\circ(\tilde{R}^{T}\cdot-)\big)^{-1}}\bullet\overline{\bar{\phi}^{\mathrm{v}}}^{\mathrm{h}}.

The statement then follows once we can pass
\tilde{R}\circ-^{\text{op}}\cong-^{\text{op}}\circ(\tilde{R}^{T}\cdot-)
, but this is precisely the naturality of
\tilde{R}
against
\tilde{S}_{v}=-^{\text{op}}
mentioned in §
5.3
.
∎

6.2.2
Extending the *-operations to the lattice 2-algebra

We are now in a position to extend this *-operation to the entirety of
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
. In order to do so, we must prove that the relations, namely the covariance condition (
6.1
) and the braiding relation (
6.3
), must be preserved.

Proposition 6.12
.

The *-operations preserves the left-covariance condition (
6.1
) (and hence also the right-covariance condition).

Proof.

By
Proposition
6.2
, it suffice to prove the statement for left-covariance. We will do this for the horizontal and the vertical orientation reversals at the same time. Towards this, let us introduce the notation
-^{*}
to denote either
*_{1}
or
*_{2}
, and denote
\bar{\phi},\bar{\Lambda}
the evaluation of 2-graph operators on, and the 2-gauge transformations in the proximity of, the corresponding orientation reversed 2-graphs. The only caveat is that for 2-gauge transformations, the *-operation
\zeta\mapsto\zeta^{*}
comes with a
1-graph
orientation reversal.

We will treat
-^{*}
as a anti-homomorphism also for the semidirect product structure
\lhd
for
\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}
; see §
6.1.1
. Recall
\tilde{\Delta}^{\text{op}}=\sigma\tilde{\Delta}
, we then compute for each
\zeta\in\tilde{{\cal C}}
and right-covariant
\phi\in\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
, using the intertwining properties (
5.4
) (and neglecting the
\mathrm{h}
-subscripts),

\displaystyle((1\times U)_{\tilde{\Delta}(\zeta)}\bullet\phi)^{*}

\displaystyle\cong\phi^{*}\bullet(1\times U)_{\tilde{\Delta}^{\text{op}}({\zeta^{*}})}

\displaystyle\cong\bar{\phi}((-\bullet(1\times\bar{U})_{\tilde{R}^{-1}})\circ\eta\circ(-\bullet(1\times U)_{(\sigma\tilde{\Delta})(\zeta^{*})}))

\displaystyle\cong(\bar{\phi}\bullet(1\times\bar{U})_{\tilde{R}^{-1}\cdot(\sigma\tilde{\Delta})({\zeta^{*}})})\eta

\displaystyle\cong(\bar{\phi}\bullet(1\times\bar{U})_{\tilde{\Delta}({\zeta^{*}})\cdot\tilde{R}^{-1}})\eta\cong(\zeta^{*}\bullet\bar{\phi})\bullet(1\times\bar{U})_{\tilde{R}}\eta

\displaystyle\cong\zeta^{*}\bullet(\bar{\phi}\bullet(1\times\bar{U})_{\tilde{R}^{-1}}\eta)=\zeta^{*}\bullet\phi^{*},

where in the fourth line we have used the right-covariance property (
6.1
) and in the fifth line a bimodule associator
(-\bullet\phi)\bullet-\cong-\bullet(\phi\bullet-)
.
∎

We now also need to show that these *-operations preserves the braid relation (
6.3
). This can be done in a completely analogous way as in the latter half of the proof of Lemma 8 in
[
2
]
. To do this, we first note that the quantum
R
-matrices
\tilde{R}
are compatible with the antipode. Then, by making use of the left-covariance property to pass
\tilde{R}
to the left,

\phi\bullet(1\times\Lambda)_{\tilde{R}^{-1}}\cong(1\times\Lambda\times\Lambda)_{(1\otimes\tilde{\Delta})\tilde{R}^{-1}}\bullet\phi,

a series of computations similar to the above proposition can be performed to show that the *-operation indeed preserves the braid relations.

The compatibility between the *-operation and the bimodule structure
\bullet
then implies that the invariance condition (
6.4
) is also preserved.

Theorem 6.13
.

The above *-operation extends to strongly-commutative functors

-^{*_{1}}:\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\Gamma})^{\text{m-op},\text{c-op}},\qquad,-^{*_{2}}:\mathscr{B}^{\Gamma}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\Gamma})^{\text{op}}.

Further, they descend to the observables
\mathscr{O}^{\Gamma}
.

This result is important for constructing scattering amplitudes on the lattice in a future work.

7
Conclusion

Given a Lie 2-group
\mathbb{G}
, this paper lays the foundation upon which the 4d 2-Chern-Simons theory can be quantized on the lattice. Based on the notion of measureable categories, we have introduced structures which capture the kinematical lattice degrees of freedom of the theory, and categoriefied the notion of quantum groups to the context of Hopf categories. This substantiates the expectations from the categorical ladder proposal of Baez-Dolan
[
16
]
.

Based on the framework introduced in this paper, we provided a categorified notion of quantum groups, and described their Hopf (co)categorical structures. These are higher structures internal to the 2-category
\mathsf{Meas}
(or the non-commutative version
\mathsf{Meas}_{q}
; see §
4.3
), the measureable categories of Crane-Yetter, which are the natural background for the representation theory of Lie 2-groups
[
56
,
183
,
10
]
. We have also shown how, under certain technical assumptions, these categorical quantum group reduces to the known Lie 2-bialgebra symmetries of the 2-Chern-Simons action.

As mentioned in the introduction, this work is part of a series towards the computation of 4-simplex scattering amplitudes for 2-Chern-Simons theory, and this shall remain the central goal. Towards this, the companion paper
[
48
]
examines the geometric,
SO(3)
-volutive aspects of the 2-representations of the categorified quantum enveloping algebra, and show that they form, in a suitable sense, a
ribbon tensor 2-category
. Future work in the series will construct lattice scattering amplitudes from these 2-representations in order to resolve
Conjecture
1.2
.

We have also made numerous comments about how our framework can be directly applied to quantize the weak/semistrict 2-Chern-Simons theory
[
161
]
on the lattice. The resulting lattice scattering amplitudes would serve as a vast generalization beyond the familiar 4d Crane-Yetter TQFT.

Higher-integrable boundaries of 2-Chern-Simons theory

An interesting prospect is to study the boundary theories of 2-Chern-Simons theory through this lattice theoretic, categorical approach. Recent works
[
46
,
156
]
have examined the analogue of the localization procedure of Costello-Yamazaki
[
53
]
for 2-Chern-Simons theory, and they seem to host
derived
current algebras as studied in the literature
[
72
,
103
,
87
,
4
,
3
]
. It would therefore be interesting to study how representations of
\mathbb{U}_{q}\mathfrak{G}
are related to the operator algebras of these 3d boundary theories,

Appendix A
Hopf 2-algebras and the necessity of categorification

The central goal of this paper is to construct, in the combinatorial setting, the quantized algebra of observables in the 2-Chern-Simons theory. If we begin from the semiclassical perspective as inspired by
[
66
,
98
]
, then we are prompted to consider a quantization of the Lie 2-bialgebra symmetries (and its classical 2-
r
-matrix)
[
24
,
42
]
underlying the 2-Chern-Simons action.

This led to the development of
Hopf 2-algebras
, which had appeared in various guises in the literature
[
171
,
44
,
122
,
130
]
. The formulation which we shall consider are 2-term
Hopf
A_{\infty}
-algebras
, fitting into the following diagram

Lie bialgberas
Hopf algberas
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L_{\infty}\text{-bialgebras}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\text{Hopf }A_{\infty}\text{-algebras}}}

for which one can describe an analogue of the "universal envelop" of the Lie 2-algebra
\mathfrak{G}=\mathfrak{h}\xrightarrow{\mu_{1}}\mathfrak{g}
.

A.1
Universal enveloping 2-algebra

We first pin down the structures we wish to study.

Definition A.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathbb{G}=\mathfrak{h}\xrightarrow{\mu_{1}}\mathfrak{g}
denote the Lie 2-algebra associated to the Lie 2-group
\mathbb{G}
. The
universal enveloping algebra
of
\mathfrak{G}
,

U\mathfrak{G}=U\mathfrak{h}\xrightarrow{D\mu_{1}}U\mathfrak{g},

is the tensor
A_{2}
-algebra (ie. a 2-term chain complex with differential graded algebra structure)

\bigoplus_{n}\mathfrak{h}^{\otimes n}\xrightarrow{D_{\mu_{1}}}\bigoplus_{n}\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n},\qquad D_{\mu_{1}}=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i-1}1\otimes\dots\otimes\mu_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes 1

freely generated by the Lie algebras
\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}
, subject to the following relations on homogeneous elements

\displaystyle x\otimes x^{\prime}-x^{\prime}\otimes x=[x,x^{\prime}],\qquad x,x^{\prime}\in U\mathfrak{g},

\displaystyle y\otimes y^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\otimes y=[y,y^{\prime}],\qquad y,y^{\prime}\in U\mathfrak{h},

\displaystyle x\otimes y-y\otimes x=\mu_{2}(x,y),\qquad x\in U\mathfrak{g},\penalty\ y\in U\mathfrak{h},

where
x\in\mathfrak{g}\subset U\mathfrak{g}
is given the degree 1 and
y\in\mathfrak{h}\subset U\mathfrak{h}
is given a degree 2. We denote the unique element with homogeneous degree-0 by "
1
".

It can be seen that
U\mathfrak{G}
has the "minimal" amount of relations to admit a canonical injection
\iota:\mathfrak{G}\hookrightarrow U\mathfrak{G}
. This can be stated as a universal property.

Now since 2-term chain complexes can be equivalently thought of as categories internal to
\mathsf{Vect}
— aka. the so-called
Baez-Crans
2-vector spaces
\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}=\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathsf{Vect}}

[
12
]
, the following construction was given in
[
44
]
.

Proposition A.2
.

U\mathfrak{G}
is a
Hopf 2-algebra
: namely a Hopf category internal to
\mathsf{Vect}
.

The theory of
weak

A_{\infty}
-algebras and their modules were studied in
[
44
]
. They serve as the foundation for the universal envelopes
U\mathfrak{G}
of the so-called weak Lie 2-algebras
[
50
]
.

Remark A.1
.

Without specifying the coproduct, the above construction has also appeared previously in
[
171
]
as a "universal enveloping functor"

U:\mathsf{Lie2Alg}\rightarrow\mathsf{2Alg},\qquad\mathfrak{G}\mapsto U\mathfrak{G}

which sends a Lie 2-algebra to a graded associative (ie.
A_{2}
-)algebra. Since (Lie) 2-algebras are equivalent to (Lie) algebra objects in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathsf{Vect}}=\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}

[
12
]
, we can understand
U
as a (strict) functor of internal categories.

\Diamond

We can prove the graded analogue of the following property of the universal enveloping algebra
[
93
]
.

Proposition A.3
.

There is a canonical isomorphism of
A_{2}
-algebras
U\mathfrak{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Diff}(\mathbb{G})^{\mathbb{G}}
, where
\operatorname{Diff}(\mathbb{G})^{\mathbb{G}}
is the
A_{2}
-algebra of invariant (under both group and groupoid multiplication) derivations on the
A_{2}
-algebra of functions
C(\mathbb{G})
on
\mathbb{G}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{G}}\in\mathsf{Lie2Alg}
denote respectively the
L_{2}
-algebras of left-invariant (again, under both group and groupoid multiplication) vector fields on
\mathbb{G}
, and
\operatorname{Der}(\mathbb{G})\in\mathsf{Lie2Alg}
the graded derivations on the functions
C(\mathbb{G})
.

The fact that we have a canonical
L_{2}
-algebra isomorphism
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{G}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Der}(\mathbb{G})
is known in
[
49
]
. At the same time,
\mathfrak{G}=\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{G}}|_{(1,1)}
is by definition the left-invariant vectors over the 2-group unit
(1,1)=(1_{\mathsf{H}},1_{G})\in\mathbb{G}
, hence this
L_{2}
-algebra isomorphism restricts to
\mathfrak{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Der}(\mathbb{G})^{\mathbb{G}}
, where the target
\operatorname{Der}(\mathbb{G})^{\mathbb{G}}
consist of the
\mathbb{G}
-invariant derivations on
C(\mathbb{G})
.

Define
\operatorname{Diff}(\mathbb{G})^{\mathbb{G}}\in\mathsf{2Alg}
the
A_{2}
-algebra of invariant differential operators on
C(\mathbb{G})
. By applying the functor
U
, the above
L_{2}
-isomorphism lifts to a
A_{2}
-algebra homomorphism
U\mathfrak{G}\rightarrow\operatorname{Diff}(\mathbb{G})^{\mathbb{G}}
. It is not hard to show that this map is bijective.
∎

This is how
U\mathfrak{G}
(or
U_{q}\mathfrak{G}
) acts canonically on
C(\mathbb{G})
(or
C_{q}(\mathbb{G})
). This fact was used in §
4.2
.

There is a left adjoint functor to the universal envelop
U
defined in
Remark
A.1
, which is the
Lie-ification
functor
L:\mathsf{2Alg}\rightarrow\mathsf{Lie2Alg}

[
171
]
. It was proven in the appendix B of
[
44
]
that, if
(A;R)
is a Hopf 2-algebra equipped with a
R
-matrix (see §
4.2.1
), then
L(A,R)=(L(A);r)
is a Lie 2-bialgebra equipped with a classical 2-
r
-matrix.

A.2
Problems with Hopf 2-algebras

The formulation above is fine by itself, but there are several problems suffered by Baez-Crans 2-vector spaces (i) in regards to its modules, and (ii) in regards to its additivity structures.

A.2.1
Representation theory in
\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}
and additivity at all levels

By definition, a module
V\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}}(A)
of an
A_{2}
-algebra
A
is a Baez-Crans 2-vector space
V\in\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}
equipped with an
A_{2}
-algebra map
\rho:A\rightarrow\operatorname{End}(V)
, where
\operatorname{End}(V)
is the endomorphism 2-algebra
[
7
]
. It was proven in
[
44
]
that the 2-category
\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathsf{2Vect}^{hBC}}(A)
of modules of a Hopf 2-algebra
A
, equipped with a 2-
R
-matrix. is
braided monoidal

[
11
,
92
,
138
]
.

However, such 2-representations are known to not carry any non-trivial
k
-invariants
[
95
]
; that is, all modules of
A
are isomorphic to a trivial one. A generalization that remedies this issue was introduced in
[
44
]
, which consist of
\mathsf{Vect}
-internal categories whose algebra objects are
lax
(ie.
pseudo
algebras
[
75
]
) — namely the (Lie) algebra objects are 2-term (
L_{\infty}
-)
A_{\infty}
-algebras
[
165
]
.

This led to the definition of the
homotopy refinement

\mathsf{2Vect}^{hBC}
of Bae-Crans 2-vector spaces, and it can be shown that the modules
V\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathsf{2Vect}^{hBC}}(A)
of a 2-term Hopf
A_{\infty}
-algebra do indeed carry non-trivial
k
-invariants in .

The second problem concerning additivity, on the other hand, is a separate issue. When viewed as
\mathsf{Vect}
-internal categories in
\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}
(or even
\mathsf{Vect}
-internal psedo-catgories in
\mathsf{2Vect}^{hBC}
), the composition of their morphisms are given simply by vector space
addition

x\xrightarrow{y}x^{\prime}\xrightarrow{y^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}=x\xrightarrow{y+y^{\prime\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}.

This has the following unsavoury consequence: that a
\mathsf{Vect}
-internal category
A=A_{1}\otimes A_{0}\rightrightarrows A_{0}
cannot be made additive. Since the origin
0\in A_{1}
already occupies the role of the unit morphism over
A_{0}
, there is no way to define a "zero morphism".

This is of particular problem for the group 2-algebra
A=\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{G}]
of a finite 2-group
\mathbb{G}
specifically. Indeed, one cannot view
A
as a category internal to
\mathsf{Vect}
while simultaneously keeping the
multiplicative
groupoid prdouct in
\mathbb{G}
as the composition in
A
. We either have to forgo the Baez-Crans 2-vector space formulation altogether (cf. the (2-)groupoid linearization in
[
37
]
), or one must perform a highly non-canonical quotient
[
171
,
44
]
.

A.2.2
Quantum states and correlation functions of Wilson surfaces

The above is not just a mathematical problem, but in fact a physical one. Indeed, if we take inspiration from
[
2
,
178
,
167
]
, then "Hopf 2-algebras" should model the structure of the
correlation functions
in 2-gauge theory. To be more explicit, consider the surface-/path-ordered 2-holonomies
[
23
,
158
,
181
,
46
]

S\exp\int_{\Sigma}B,\penalty\ P\exp\int_{\gamma}A
, which can be written as a 2-functor (see also
[
109
]
)

P^{2}M^{4}\rightarrow(\mathbb{G},\ast),\qquad(\Sigma,\gamma)\mapsto\left(S\exp\int_{\Sigma}B,\penalty\ P\exp\int_{\gamma}A\right)

2-path groupoid
P^{2}M^{4}
(also called the "surface 2-groupoid") on
M^{4}
. Given some appropriate categorical notion of "2-trace"
[
85
,
86
,
26
,
96
]
associated to a higher irreducible representation
\rho
of
\mathbb{G}
, the associated Wilson surface correlation functions can be formally write as a path integral (see also
[
116
]
for the finite 2-group case)

\langle W_{\rho}(\Sigma,\gamma)\rangle=\frac{1}{Z}\int D[A,B]\operatorname{Tr}_{\rho}\left[\left(S\exp\int_{\Sigma}B\right)\cdot\left(P\exp\int_{\gamma}A\right)\right]e^{i2\pi kS_{2CS}[A,B]},

which should inherit the
multiplicative
gluing composition
\Sigma\circ\Sigma^{\prime}=\Sigma\cup_{\gamma}\Sigma^{\prime}
of surfaces from the 2-holonomies.

However, as we have seen above, one cannot keep both additivity and such multiplicative vertical products in the framework of Baez-Crans 2-vector spaces — Wilson surface correlators
cannot
be modelled by Hopf 2-algebras!

The point of categorifying to the context of the measureable categories
\mathsf{Meas}
, as well as the technical conditions in "Hypothesis (H)"
Definition
4.13
, is to remedy this problem. The decategorification 2-functor
\lambda:\mathsf{Cat}_{\mathsf{Meas}}\rightarrow\mathsf{Cat}_{\mathsf{Vect}}=\mathsf{2Vect}^{BC}
serves as a way to lift Hopf 2-algebras to the structure of the 2-graph operators
\mathfrak{C}_{q}(\mathbb{G}^{\Gamma})
, which has enough room for multiplicative composition laws without sacrificing additivity.

We also mention that the choice
\mathsf{Meas}
is not random. The measureable categories are the infinite-dimensional analogues of Baez’s 2-Hilbert spaces
\mathsf{2Hilb}

[
17
]
, and it serves as the natural backdrop for the representation theory of
Lie
2-groups
[
56
,
10
]
and Lie groupoids
[
168
]
. As we have mentioned in §
4.3
,
\mathsf{Meas}
is also closely related to the frameworks of
[
94
,
113
]
.
